Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope it's okay that I'm here. I guess I came in because... well, I've been trying to clean up my image and my routine lately for this arts board stuff I do. And I thought it would be a really positive thing, but I've actually been feeling a bit more tense and... maybe a little down? Just more than I expected to be. 

I mean, I'm still doing the basics, I promise I'm keeping up with the main stuff! But I just keep dropping these little things I usually really care about, like my fair planning notes or my design drafts. And then I feel so guilty about it that I end up canceling plans with people instead of just catching up on the work. 

I'm sorry, I know it probably sounds a bit silly, and I'm sure you see people with much bigger problems, but... does that make sense for why I'd be here?

Well, um... I guess that's sort of why I decided to come in. I mean, it's not like a huge, terrible tragedy or anything! Just... I've been really trying to clean up my image and my whole routine for this arts board I'm on. I want to make a good impression, you know?

Oh, no, I don't think so. It's really not about things needing to be perfect! I mean, I know I mentioned canceling some social plans earlier, but that's mostly just because I feel bad about falling behind on my work, not because I'm afraid of doing things imperfectly. 

I always try my best to just... show up and do what I need to do, especially for the board, even if my work isn't absolutely flawless. I know nobody's perfect, and I definitely wouldn't want to let people down by just avoiding a task altogether just because I'm nervous about it. I think it's much better to just give it your best effort rather than hide away from it. That's a good mindset to have, right? Just doing your best and being okay with it?

Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, sure, everyone has moments where they doubt themselves a little bit, right? But I don't walk around feeling like I'm not good enough in general.

Oh, sleep? No, I've been sleeping fine, really. I mean, maybe it takes me a tiny bit longer to drift off sometimes if I have a lot on my mind, but that's normal, right? Everyone has nights where they think about their day a little bit before they fall asleep.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess... maybe about two months? It really hasn't been that long. 

It started right around the time I decided to, you know, overhaul my daily routine and how I present myself. I just wanted to make some positive changes and put my best foot forward while I'm figuring things out. So, naturally, adjusting to all those new habits has just made me feel a little bit tired or off some days. But two months isn't really that long to be adjusting to a new schedule, is it? I feel like it takes anyone a little time to get used to a big routine change, right?
